# InterSidera: plan and durable memory

## 1. Status

- Milestone: **M7 Delivery done 2026-09-30**: the eight milestones are done (M7 with the acceptance of plan D196 run twice on `git clone`s, of its milestone commit and of the fix commit that acceptance called for, and the commit `docs(plan): M7 acceptance record and final report`, which ticks the three Definition-of-done items of section 6; M6 done 2026-09-24, M5 done 2026-09-17, M4 done 2026-09-09, M3 done 2026-09-06, M2 done 2026-09-06, M1 done 2026-09-03, M0 done 2026-09-02); the commit `build: docker compose, images and release checklist` (the two images and their gates, the nginx edge, `compose.yaml` with the TLS and acceptance overlays, `make check-delivery`/`images`/`up`/`down`, the CI `docker` job, the runbook and the release checklist in `docs/architecture.md` "Deployment", ADR-0023..0025 and the amendments of ADR-0003, 0005, 0008, 0017, 0018; `make check` green on 2026-09-30: pytest 694 not-slow, vitest 990 in 110 files, i18n 436 keys, `check-delivery` 694 checks / 0 findings / self-test 19/19; `make images` on arm64 on 2026-09-30: `check_images` 254 assertions, 0 findings, api 394,364,581 B and web 16,829,111 B unpacked (245 assertions and api 394,364,527 B on 2026-09-28, before the review's fix pass tightened checks (e) and (g) and re-wrapped one docstring); the stack rehearsal and the image measurements are dated 2026-09-28, the re-proofs after the fix pass 2026-09-30); the acceptance record (16 rows) and the final report follow in the commit `docs(plan): M7 acceptance record and final report` (plan D196). The acceptance run of 2026-09-30 on a clone of that commit passed every scripted step, and the adversarial check of its evidence found three defects fixed in `fix(frontend): keep lang in the URL, signed fields, © attribution` (2026-09-30, `7ee936b`, the log below); the acceptance re-run on a clone of that commit (2026-09-30, plan D196): `docker compose run --rm api sky-data fetch` 99.5 s, `docker compose up -d --wait` 42.6 s to `ready` on a fresh data directory, the 28 probes, the e2e suite through nginx 60 passed / 8 skipped / 0 failed in 11.0 min and Lighthouse 1.00 x 6, the native `make check`, `make test`, `make e2e` and `make build`, the de441 stage (ready 4.79 s after the start), `make images` and `make audit` in the clone, the one-off observations of `docs/testing.md`; the acceptance record (16 rows) and the final report close section 6 (plan D195). The M6 status, kept as history: **M6 Hardening done 2026-09-24**; the `build(deps): refresh lockfiles within ranges` commit of 2026-09-23 (Step 0, plan D164) precedes the milestone commit `chore: hardening, performance and documentation` (2026-09-27; `make check` 694 pytest / 990 vitest / 436 keys, `make test` 695, `make build` 421.8 kB gzip eager); next: M7 (plan mode first, per brief l.504).
- Next (after M7, every item human-only; section 11 "Human-only items after M6 and M7"): `git push` and the first remote CI run (the `docker` job builds the linux/amd64 images on the runner and prints their `check_images.mjs` record lines, `docker build --check` on the runner's Docker 28.0.4, the Lighthouse step, the weekly `audit` job, then the manual `webkit` and `chromium-webgpu` one-offs); real certificates and the TLS deployment choice (nginx in tls mode or an external proxy, HSTS ownership, never `preload`); the production `.env` values (`SKYAPI_EPHEMERIS=de441.bsp`, `SKYAPI_WORKERS`, `SKY_API_MEMORY_LIMIT`, `SKYAPI_CORS_ORIGINS`, `SKYAPI_GEOCODER_EMAIL`, `SKY_GEOCODER_ORIGIN`); the phone rows and the AR device checklist; answers to maintainer questions 1, 23 and 25-54 (54, the editor's TT - UT at far dates, is what the acceptance added: B-112). The M6 hand-off list that follows is kept for the record (each item's M7 outcome is under section 6 "M7 Delivery", Amended items). What M7 received from M6: the nginx template applies the header set of `frontend/security-headers.ts` (`add_header ... always` at server level, `connect-src 'self' ${SKY_GEOCODER_ORIGIN}`, `upgrade-insecure-requests` and `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains` in the TLS block, never `preload` without the maintainer, `Cache-Control` per asset class: `index.html` `no-cache`, `/assets/*` `public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, favicon and manifest `max-age=3600`), `location ~ /\. { return 404; }` so `dist/.vite/manifest.json` never ships and the web image copies `dist/` without `.vite/`, `location = /api/v1/docs { return 404; }` (D150, B-95), `proxy_hide_header Server` and `server_tokens off` (D166), `ETag` kept on `/api/v1/catalogs/*` (the 304 rows of `url-state.spec.ts`), the SKYS served uncompressed or through `gzip_static` (never gzip on the fly, B-46; compression and byte ranges are exclusive, B-84), the double CSP on `/api` (the API's own `nosniff` and deny policy under nginx's set, D149), a `scripts/check_nginx_headers.mjs` diff against the module; `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS=<compose network>` for the rate-limit key (`.env.example`, D166); a container memory limit above 1 GB for a worker serving concurrent 100-body frames, or a bounded minor-body concurrency in the backend (R114); the release checklist items: `SKY_GEOCODER_ORIGIN` equal to the origin of `SKYAPI_GEOCODER_URL` (R104), HSTS ownership (nginx or an external TLS proxy), the public `/meta.geocoder.email`; the pending rows of the `docs/testing.md` budgets table (the phone, de441, the images: D167) and maintainer questions 29-34. The M5-era list that follows is kept for the record: performance passes (bundle analysis after the M5 `$initial` regrouping, frame-time profiling on a phone incl. the AR modes: R78, R79, Q57), accessibility audit (Lighthouse >= 90; `@axe-core/playwright` needs an ADR, maintainer question 23), CSP and security headers (the camera, sensors and WebXR need no new origin; `.vite/manifest.json` must not ship), `pip-audit`/`npm audit` with an allow-list (the js-yaml note of the M4 review), error paths, i18n completeness, the M5 follow-ups Q49-Q51 and Q62-Q65, labels inside the XR overlay (Q62), the `babylon-webgpu` regex (Q63, R96), a `NullEngine` test of `XrBridge` (Q65), the first-attempt flake of the M4 follow-mode e2e assertion (R95), the camera-lost-in-XR path on a real phone (R94, checklist rows 18 and 22), an `onRetry` publication (R70), boundary densification (R73), ADRs for every notable decision, README quickstart, documentation review, backlog. What M6 consumes from M5: `scripts/check_chunks.mjs` and `build.manifest` (the lazy-chunk gate), the `ar` slice and the device checklist rows the maintainer records (question 25), the WebGPU-versus-WebXR policy (question 26), the blend decision (question 27) and the phone TLS path (question 28).
- Specification: `docs/brief.xml` v1.2 (587 lines). Precedence when sections conflict (brief l.11): legal and licensing > security and privacy > api_contract > architecture > [M] functional > non-functional budgets > [S] > style.

How to use this file (brief l.507: it is the durable memory across sessions):

- At the start of every session read sections 1, 6 and 12, then the sections a task touches (3 and 7 before changing a pin, 5 before touching a data URL, 4 and 10 before deviating from the brief).
- Tick checkboxes in section 6 and append one line to section 12 in the same commit as the change. Never rewrite past entries.
- Decide-and-continue for non-blocking questions: record the decision in section 10; questions that need the maintainer go to section 11. Ask the human only for the four blocking cases of brief l.513.
- Every deviation from the brief also gets a row in `docs/backlog.md`; every notable decision an ADR in `docs/decisions/`.

| D188 | Proxy trust: `x-sky: {subnet: &sky_subnet 10.213.0.0/24}` written once (outside Docker's default pools and WSL 2's NAT range); `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` = that network (zero host bits: uvicorn's strict `ip_network` degrades a bad value to a never-matching literal); nginx sends `X-Forwarded-For $proxy_add_x_forwarded_for`; uvicorn takes the first untrusted address from the right, so remote clients cannot forge the key (the docker host itself, gateway `10.213.0.1`, is trusted: accepted); a collision -> `compose.override.yaml` changes both uses. Amended 2026-09-28 (the M7 fix pass; review finding 1, MAJOR): the trusted set is `web`'s fixed address, not the network: `x-sky: {subnet: &sky_subnet 10.213.0.0/24, web_address: &sky_web 10.213.0.3}`, `web.networks.default.ipv4_address: *sky_web`, `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS: *sky_web` (never the /24, never `*`); the sentence "the docker host itself, gateway `10.213.0.1`, is trusted: accepted" is superseded, because the gateway relays every host connection and, behind Docker Desktop's userland proxy (and, per Docker's port-publishing documentation, for the IPv6 clients of a port published without a host address on the IPv4-only network), every published-port connection: with the /24 trusted the rehearsal's probe 16 saw a forged `X-Forwarded-For` answer 200 while unforged requests answered 429; with the address alone uvicorn walks the header from the right and stops at the first untrusted address, the one nginx itself appended (`$proxy_add_x_forwarded_for`: the real client on a Linux host, the gateway behind Docker Desktop), so a client-supplied entry is never reached and a forged header is keyed like an unforged one; an external TLS proxy whose header must be honoured is appended after the web address in `compose.override.yaml` (its address as nginx sees it, D182); in `compose.e2e.yaml` the published 8000 lets host clients reach uvicorn directly from the untrusted gateway, so their header is ignored (loopback only, acceptance only); a collision now changes every use (the subnet, the address, the variable); nginx and `scripts/check_nginx_headers.mjs` unchanged; probes 16 and 21 re-measured on 2026-09-30 on the stack of the milestone tree (ADR-0025 amendment): `web` at `10.213.0.3`, the api at `10.213.0.2`, `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS` `10.213.0.3`; after a same-process burst emptied the host bucket (1,500 requests in 2.04 s: 80 x 200 / 1,420 x 429) six requests interleaved forged (`X-Forwarded-For: 203.0.113.7`), plain, forged, plain, forged, plain answered `429 429 429 429 429 429`, three tails out of three; the control with the /24 trusted again through a scratch override answered `200 429 200 429 200 429` with the same script (the pre-fix behaviour: the forged requests 200 three times out of three in each of three tails, one refill 200 among the plain ones), and the committed file `429` x 6 again; Protocol note 2026-09-28: run 2 does not run `sky-data update` (the plan's section 5 row for l.298 asked for its exit 0 in run 2; it would re-download MPCORB and rebuild the caches in the shared 3.95 GB directory, a network operation outside the acceptance's scope): `docker compose run --rm api sky-data update --help` exits 0 in the container and stands in, and the weekly update is the operator's job (a protocol choice, not a brief deviation: no backlog row). Protocol note 2026-09-30: run 1 uses the clone's own empty `./data` (`.env` = `.env.example` unmodified, `SKY_DATA_DIR=./data`; the directory `data/.gitkeep` gives the clone, D194) instead of a separate scratch directory, so the two commands of brief l.490 run exactly as a clean clone runs them; `make setup` runs first in the clone (its `npm ci` serves the Playwright run through nginx); `sky-data verify` runs after the fetch and again after the first start (the second run also checks the caches the bootstrap built); the `__sky` grep on the production image goes through plain `docker run` (a `docker compose run web` would claim `web`'s fixed address while the stack is up); Lighthouse (`scripts/lighthouse.mjs`, six URLs) is also run through nginx on the e2e overlay. Outcome 2026-09-30: run once on a clone of `0beb9ee` and, after the fix commit `7ee936b` its evidence check called for, again on a clone of that commit (the run of record; in the record, the tables and the amendments "run 1" and "run 2" name these two clones, and the de440s and de441 parts of this protocol are called its stages); as run the protocol also had a `docker compose build --no-cache` with a forced re-creation of the stack before the probes, an extended sample of probe 16, the API's acceptance items through nginx, `docker compose build --pull`, `make audit`, a pause for a load average under 1.5 before the native `make e2e` with a fall-back to the CI settings after a five-worker starvation failure, and the one-off observations of `docs/testing.md`; every step passed in both runs (the native `make e2e` of the first clone at its second attempt: 57 passed / 1 failed / 8 skipped right after `make test`, then 58 passed / 8 skipped / 0 failed alone; the second clone 56 passed / 4 failed / 8 skipped at five workers, then 60 passed / 8 skipped / 0 failed in 18.8 min under the CI settings); the figures are in `docs/testing.md` "Delivery (M7)", the record and the final report in section 6 | l.416, l.490-492, l.517, l.564, l.584; testing.md l.240 | ADR-0025 |

## 4. Deviations from the brief's wording

Each row also exists in `docs/backlog.md` with the same identifier.

| MPCORB size                         | "~250 MB" (l.19, l.299)                                                    | 317,106,824 B inflated (93,611,374 B gzipped) on 2026-09-03                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | download                                                                                                                                                                                           | sizes documented in `docs/data.md`                                                                                      |
| `pck00010.tpc` fallback             | fall back to `pck00010.tpc` (l.308)                                        | lives at `pck/pck00010.tpc` (126,143 B); the `a_old_versions/` path is 404; it encodes the IAU 2009 models Horizons uses                                                                                                                                                       | HEAD 2026-09-03                                                                                                                                                                                    | registry entry `optional`; maintainer question 11                                                                       |
| Horizons delta T                    | planets and Moon within 2 arcsec at any epoch (l.180, l.262)                | Horizons holds delta T constant beyond its ~73-day EOP prediction and uses Stephenson/Morrison before 1962; Skyfield uses the S15 splines and the 2016 parabola: 991 arcsec of Earth az/el at 2140; Horizons' quantity 30 is TDB - UT1 before 1962 and TDB - UTC afterwards            | Horizons manual and fixture                                                                                                                                                                        | epoch policy D37/R37; alt/az within 0.56 arcsec with Horizons' delta T injected at the far epochs                       |
| Horizons planet rotation            | (implicit)                                                                 | the manual says "The IAU2009 rotation models are used except for Earth and MOON", but Horizons' Mars pole (quantity 32) equals the pck00011 IAU 2015 pole within 0.017 arcsec at every fixture epoch                                                                              | Horizons fixture, 2026-09-03                                                                                                                                                                       | no tolerance change; B-36 closed                                                                                        |

## 6. Milestones

Rules (brief l.437): milestones run in order; each ends with `make check` green, documentation updated and a local commit. Items are ticked in the commit that completes them.

### Acceptance record (M7, brief l.560-586)

Two runs on 2026-09-30, each on a real `git clone` under `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=intersidera-acc` (plan D196; the steps, commands and figures are in `docs/testing.md` "Delivery (M7)", "Acceptance runs", the probe outcomes in the last column of its probe table, the one-off observations in "Observations"). The first run, on the milestone commit `0beb9ee`, passed every scripted step (its native `make e2e` at the second attempt, run alone: R152); the adversarial check of the evidence behind each item below (five readers over the tests, five verifiers re-opening every cited assertion) then found three defects no gate covered (the language lost on a French browser, the phone keypads of the signed fields, the "(c)" attribution), fixed in `7ee936b` with their tests; the second run, on that commit, is the run of record and its figures appear below. Result is `pass`, or `pass with exception` when a part of the item cannot be proven by the agent on this machine and is handed to the maintainer (section 11, "Human-only items after M6 and M7"). 16 items: 2 repository, 4 backend, 9 frontend, 1 delivery.

### Final report (M7, brief l.517)

Written on 2026-09-30 after the two acceptance runs of plan D196: on a `git clone` of the milestone commit `0beb9ee`, whose evidence check found three defects, and on a `git clone` of the fix commit `7ee936b`, the run of record; the acceptance record above holds the evidence per brief line, `docs/testing.md` "Delivery (M7)" the figures.

**Delivered scope.** Eight milestones in eleven local Conventional Commits on `master`, nothing pushed:

| Milestone | Commit | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M0 | `9aca0b0` chore: bootstrap monorepo (backend, frontend, tooling, ci) | the uv + FastAPI and Vite + React skeletons, the Makefile, the CI workflow, CLAUDE.md and the rules, this plan |
| M1 | `3d4d30d` feat(backend): data pipeline and astronomy core | `sky-data`, the registry with its pins and licenses, the SKYS, DSO, constellation and MPC builders, the Skyfield core with our IAU rotation frames; 360 Horizons cases within 2 arcsec |
| M2 | `ede1314` feat(api): v1 endpoints, caching, rate limiting and health | the ten routes of the contract, RFC 9457 problems, canonicalization, ETags, the bootstrap thread with its health states, rate limiting, JSON logs |
| M3 | `e8fbf0d` feat(frontend): sky engine, catalogs and core rendering | the Babylon engine on WebGL2 and WebGPU, the GLSL and WGSL shader pairs, the frame buffer, the URL as the only state |
| M4 | `cc19170` feat(frontend): user interface, search, constellations and i18n | the panels, deep-sky and minor bodies, constellations, labels, selection and details, search, night mode, French and English |
| M5 | `29b1db4` feat(frontend): augmented reality (sensor and WebXR modes) | the sensor mode over the rear camera, WebXR, the lazy chunks |
| M6 | `bf3bb2e` build(deps): refresh lockfiles within ranges; `f3efd49` chore: hardening, performance and documentation | the strict CSP and the header set, the audits, the WCAG 2.2 AA fixes, every budget row measured, ADR-0010..0022 |
| M7 | `0beb9ee` build: docker compose, images and release checklist; `7ee936b` fix(frontend): keep lang in the URL, signed fields, © attribution; this commit, `docs(plan): M7 acceptance record and final report` | the two images and their gates, the nginx edge, compose with the TLS and acceptance overlays, the CI `docker` job, the runbook and the release checklist, ADR-0023..0025; the two acceptance runs and the three defects the first one surfaced |

Every [M] requirement of the brief is implemented. The [S] requirements all shipped: OBS-6 (12 presets), TIME-5 (the keyboard shortcuts), VIEW-2 (the field-of-view presets), VIEW-4 (follow mode), VIEW-6 (the PNG export, simplified: B-67), AR-4 (WebXR, on the WebGL2 backend: B-79) and UX-5 (the first-visit hint). The [L] items are recorded in `docs/backlog.md` and not implemented: L-01 (`/sky/events`), L-02 (rise, set and twilight times), L-03 (Milky Way and constellation artwork), L-04 (IERS Earth orientation).

**Deviations.** `docs/backlog.md` holds the 112 rows B-01..B-112, each with its reason: 99 done, 1 closed as not needed (B-36). Open after M7: B-112 (the time editor converts a typed wall time with the current window's TT - UT, hours off in antiquity on de441; maintainer question 54), B-84 (the first meaningful sky on a cold 4G connection, 5.3-5.6 s against 5 s; maintainer question 29), B-91 (residual allocations in the render path, 63-85 kB per overlay tick on SwiftShader), B-96 (the XR chunk bundles four unused controller features), B-26 (fastapi-cli's banner prints `/docs`), B-38 and B-40 (two Skyfield 1.55 deprecation filters), B-49 (Vitest stays 4.1.11 until jest-dom #738). Blocked on a human: B-24 (the Playwright system libraries need sudo; a user-space library directory works) and B-51 (headless WebGPU crashes on this WSL; the x86-64 runner is unmeasured). Deferred past M7: B-02 (TypeScript 7) and B-97 (a SKYS v2 layout). The M7 rows are B-98..B-110 (the slim nginx base, no on-the-fly gzip at the edge, the template and mode files, the compose-tier `SKY_*` variables, the two overlays, `data/.gitkeep`, the two extra make targets, the two check scripts, hadolint, the e2e build argument, the compose hardening keys, `frontend/.dockerignore`, the web healthcheck); B-111, the fallback for an api image above 400 MB, did not trigger. The acceptance found three product defects no gate covered, fixed with their tests in `7ee936b` before the second run: the language was lost on a browser preferring French (`lang` at its default was not written to the URL, so the copied link and a reload took the browser's language), the year and elevation fields asked phones for a keypad without a minus key, and the Nominatim attribution read "(c)" where the brief mandates "©". It also corrected two statements of the milestone commit, each amended where it is made: nginx answers 504 for as long as the api container is stopped and 502 only while it starts (the rehearsal had read "504 once, then 502"), and `make setup` in a clean clone installs the newest Node 24.x (v24.21.0 on 2026-09-30, where the developer tree had run its gates on v24.20.0).

**Measured budgets** (`docs/testing.md` "Budgets" and "Delivery (M7)" hold the rows with their methods and dates):

| Budget (brief line) | Target | Measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `/sky/frame` p95, n = 32, default bodies (l.256) | < 150 ms | 32.0 ms on de440s, 27.5 ms on de441 (the bench); 38.3 and 57.3 ms compute on 30-request samples in the container (both taken about half a minute after the e2e overlay came down, the second on a machine whose swap was full) |
| the same with 100 minor bodies (l.256) | < 400 ms | 334.1 ms on de440s, 298.8 ms on de441 |
| catalogs from prebuilt files (l.256) | < 50 ms | stars 22.1-22.5 ms, index 8.6-9.7, constellations 4.4-6.1; dso 40.3-42.8 ms on an idle machine, 53.4-59.6 ms under load (R115) |
| `/minor-bodies/search` (l.256) | < 50 ms | 3.6-4.6 ms |
| ready after start, data present (l.256) | < 60 s | 2.16 s on de440s and 4.69 s on de441 on the host; in the container 4.92 and 4.79 s on de441 and 2.84-3.76 s on de440s; 35.89 and 34.57 s on a first start that also builds the caches |
| RSS per worker without the page cache (l.256) | < 1 GB | 749-760 MB on de441 at concurrency 1 (`RssAnon + RssShmem`); 0.90-1.01 GB under four concurrent 100-body frames (R114), hence the 2 GB container limit, whose first-start peak measured 1.39-1.41 GB with the page cache |
| desktop frame rate, every layer (l.257) | 60 fps | 60.00 fps on WebGPU and on WebGL2 (the host GPU) |
| 2022 mid-range phone (l.257) | >= 30 fps | pending the maintainer (question 25) |
| catalog parsing (l.257) | < 300 ms | 12 ms |
| first meaningful sky (l.257) | < 2 s warm, < 5 s cold 4G | warm 0.39-0.89 s (host GPU, loopback) and 1.21-1.26 s under Fast 4G and WPT 4G (2.76 s under Slow 4G); cold 5.27-5.63 s under Fast 4G and WPT 4G (27.2 s under Slow 4G): not met (B-84) |
| main bundle (l.257) | <= 1.5 MB gzip | 421.8 kB eager, the AR, XR and WebGPU code in lazy chunks |
| session download (l.258) | <= 6 MB | 4.40 MB cold, 5.3 kB warm |
| accuracy (l.262-263) | 2 arcsec; 1 arcsec; 1 arcmin | every Horizons comparison within 2 arcsec (0.56 arcsec at worst); stars 0.007 arcsec barycentric, 0.655 arcsec apparent; interpolation 0.253 arcsec; refraction 0.057 arcmin |
| live drift (l.264) | <= 100 ms | 12-23 ms at 60 fps (the host GPU) |
| time-lapse speed (l.264) | exact to 0.1 % | within 1e-3 at 3600x (`time.spec.ts`) |
| Lighthouse accessibility (l.580) | >= 90 | 1.00 on six URLs, against `vite preview` and through nginx |
| images (l.299) | api < 400 MB, web < 50 MB | api 394.4 MB arm64 (394,364,580 B in the clone of the fix commit) and 379.5 MB amd64; web 16.8 and 16.4 MB (unpacked) |
| containers (l.270) | amd64 and arm64 | arm64 built, run and accepted here; amd64 built here under emulation, its runtime proof is the CI `docker` job after the push |

**Manual steps left to the human** (section 11 lists them as "Human-only items after M6 and M7" with their protocols, and the 54 questions):

1. `git push`, then the first remote CI run: five jobs, of which `docker` builds the linux/amd64 images on the runner and prints their sizes and `e2e` ends with Lighthouse; then the one-off `webkit` and `chromium-webgpu` projects (items 3, 4 and 9).
2. TLS: real certificates as root-owned copies of `fullchain.pem` and `privkey.pem` with `compose.tls.yaml`, or an external proxy in front of the plain-HTTP stack; the HSTS value and its owner (item 10, questions 39-41).
3. The production `.env` (`SKYAPI_EPHEMERIS=de441.bsp` after `sky-data fetch --full`, the worker count with its memory limit, the CORS origin, a public geocoder e-mail, the geocoder origin pair), the 20-item release checklist of `docs/architecture.md` "Deployment" and the weekly `sky-data update` cron (item 11).
4. The 25-row device checklist (24 augmented-reality rows plus row 25, the phone keypads of the signed fields) and the phone frame-rate rows on real devices (iOS Safari 17+, Android Chrome 120+), the Firefox and Safari rows, Lighthouse's manual audits and a screen-reader pass (items 1, 2 and 6).
5. The Nominatim usage decision before production (question 1) and the answers to the open maintainer questions (1, 23, 25-54; items 8 and 12), question 54 being the editor's TT - UT at far dates (B-112).

## 8. Risks

| #   | Risk                                                                                                                           | Mitigation / decision                                                                                                           |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| R1  | A later agent bumps TypeScript to 7 and breaks lint and codegen                                                                | `~6.0.3`, ADR-0001, CLAUDE.md do-not list, `tooling.md`; revisit when typescript-eslint supports TS 7 (after 7.1)               |
| R2  | npm 11.19 hard-fails vs warns on the two stale peers (unverified before install)                                               | ship the overrides regardless; observe at the first `npm install` and record it in section 6 Observed 2026-09-02: npm 11.19.0 hard-fails with ERESOLVE without either override; both are strictly required, zero warnings with them. |
| R3  | jsdom `canvas` peer triggers a node-canvas source build on aarch64                                                             | check `npm ls canvas` after install; Babylon tests stay in Playwright Observed 2026-09-02: `npm ls canvas` empty, no native build. |
| R4  | aarch64 lockfile missing x64 binaries makes the first CI run red                                                               | mandatory grep for the three `linux-x64-gnu` packages before committing Observed 2026-09-02: all three `linux-x64-gnu` packages present, `lockfileVersion` 3. |
| R5  | pyright needs Node and network in CI                                                                                           | `setup-node` + `~/.cache/pyright-python` cache in the backend job                                                               |
| R6  | FastAPI 0.141.1 with Starlette 1.6.0 (no upper bound) is untested upstream at release level                                    | health + snapshot tests guard; pin `starlette>=1.6,<1.7` only if it breaks                                                      |
| R7  | `filterwarnings=error` surfaces third-party DeprecationWarnings on 3.14                                                        | targeted `ignore:<msg>:<Category>:<module>` entries with a comment and a backlog line; never blanket                            |
| R8  | `license = "MIT"` string with `uv_build` 0.12.9 (unverified)                                                                   | fall back to `{ text = "MIT" }` Observed 2026-09-02: `license = "MIT"` accepted by uv_build 0.12.9 without warning. |
| R9  | `.env` or CI env leaking into tests                                                                                            | `--env-file` only on run targets (D9); autouse `clean_env` fixture Observed 2026-09-02: suite passes with deliberately malformed `SKYAPI_*` values in the environment; `skyapi.main` builds `app` lazily so importing it reads nothing. |
| R10 | Node `localhost` resolves to `::1` while uvicorn binds 127.0.0.1                                                               | every URL in configs uses `127.0.0.1`                                                                                           |
| R11 | `DATA_DIR` default `../data` is CWD-dependent                                                                                  | documented; `--directory backend` and `[tool.fastapi]` enforce the CWD; compose sets `/data` at M7                              |
| R12 | `uv run --env-file` precedence and path semantics (unverified)                                                                 | absolute `$(CURDIR)/.env`; nothing else sets `SKYAPI_*` in development                                                          |
| R13 | TS 6 `noUncheckedSideEffectImports` vs Babylon side-effect imports and `?raw` (M3)                                             | Retired 2026-09-06: TS 6 does not enable `noUncheckedSideEffectImports` by default, every Babylon side-effect import has a `.d.ts` and `vite/client` declares `*?raw`; `tsc -b` green at M3 |
| R14 | typescript-eslint `projectService` may not find `vite.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `e2e/**`                             | verify at the first lint; fallback `projectService: { allowDefaultProject: [...] }` or an explicit `project` list Observed 2026-09-02: `projectService` resolves `vite.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, `e2e/**` and `resources.d.ts` through the solution-style tsconfig; no fallback needed. |
| R15 | Playwright system libs absent on this WSL; WebGPU headless flags on arm64 unknown (M3)                                         | `sudo env "PATH=$PATH" fnm exec --using=24 npm --prefix frontend exec -- playwright install-deps` is a manual human step (B-24); e2e conditional at M0; CI Chromium only; flags measured at M3 |
| R16 | `npm create vite@latest` template drift                                                                                        | pinned `create-vite@9.2.0`; diff before deleting scaffold files                                                                 |
| R17 | `git diff --exit-code` is vacuous on untracked files                                                                           | `git ls-files --error-unmatch` first (D13); both generated files committed at M0                                                |
| R18 | pandas 3.0.5 vs Skyfield `mpc.py` `dtypes=` typo and `str`-dtype columns (not executed)                                        | pin `>=3.0,<4` at M1 with a real MPCORB smoke test; fallback `>=2.3,<3` with an ADR. Observed 2026-09-03: the typo is ignored by pandas 3 as by 2.x (verified in `readers.py`); `load_comets_dataframe_slow` and Skyfield's orbit functions run unchanged on pandas 3.0.5; no fallback needed. |
| R19 | Skyfield 1.55 not classified for Python 3.14 (PR #1100 open)                                                                   | M1 first task: `import skyfield` + de440s load on 3.14.7                                                                        |
| R20 | Skyfield #952: multi-segment Moon PCK makes Moon observers fail before 2426                                                    | M1: date-aware segment selector + pre-2426 regression test                                                                      |
| R21 | MPC `www` host unreachable from this network                                                                                   | per-dataset URL override defaulting to `data.minorplanetcenter.net`; offline fixture path                                       |

| R27 | Horizons lunar site frame differs from `MOON_ME_DE440_ME421`                                                                    | Retired: Horizons uses the DE421 mean-Earth frame as a fixed offset from the DE440 PA system, i.e. the same frame                   |
| R28 | NAIF renames `moon_de440_*.tf` again                                                                                            | registry `fallback_urls`; `verify` fails loudly; rename procedure in `docs/data.md`                                              |

| R30 | full MPCORB parse time and RSS in `build-caches`                                                                                | Observed 2026-09-03: Skyfield's single-call loader 46.9 s and 3,648 MB peak RSS on the 317 MB file; the chunked `read_fwf` reader (B-39) 22.2 s and 1,219 MB; adopted                                    |
| R31 | `uv_build` does not package non-Python files                                                                                    | Retired: the uv docs say data files under the module root ship in the wheel; verified with `uv build`                            |
| R32 | Hipparcos excerpt exceeds 1 MB                                                                                                  | ~1,000 rows of 451 B plus the constellation HIPs; asserted < 1 MB by a test                                                       |
| R33 | Horizons throttling or format change                                                                                            | manual run, 1.5 s pause, `format=json`, fixtures committed; tests never call it                                                   |
| R34 | our IAU 2015 rotation model vs Horizons' IAU 2009 for Mars sites                                                                | Retired 2026-09-03: Horizons' Mars pole (quantity 32) matches our pck00011 model within 0.017 arcsec at all six epochs (the manual's "IAU2009" statement is outdated for Mars); Jezero alt/az agree within 0.0006 arcsec once our planetocentric zenith is tilted to Horizons' planetodetic normal; B-36 not needed |
| R35 | DE441 `Stack` targets return NaN silently outside coverage                                                                      | explicit coverage checks with a one-day light-time margin; tests assert `CoverageError`, never NaN                               |
| R36 | Horizons longitude conventions (west-positive for Mars) and planetodetic latitudes                                             | non-Earth sites sent as CYLINDRICAL from our planetocentric vector; the echoed centre line is asserted                             |
| R37 | Horizons and Skyfield delta T models diverge outside the IERS table                                                             | decided in D37 and measured: only 2000 and 2024 are inside Skyfield's IERS table (1973-2027); 1900, 1969, 2050 and 2140 use Horizons' quantity 30 (TDB - UTC after 1962, UT1 - UTC <= 0.03 s there); az/el within 0.56 arcsec everywhere; the builtin timescale alone would be off by 3.8 arcsec (1969), 35 arcsec (2050) and 991 arcsec (2140) |
| R38 | `Star.at(t)` does not exist (brief l.139)                                                                                       | `SSB.at(t).observe(star)` at two epochs (D48); recorded in section 5                                                              |
| R39 | Background bootstrap thread under `TestClient`: readiness timing; kernels left open on a failure path (fatal `ResourceWarning`) | `wait_ready` polls `/health` and fails fast on `detail`; the thread body catches every exception and closes an opened `AstroState`; the lifespan cancels, joins (70 s) and closes |
| R40 | GZip of the 3.8 MB SKYS on the fly cannot meet the 50 ms catalog budget | Confirmed 2026-09-06 (level 9: 316 ms p95, level 1 still 104 ms of compression): `application/octet-stream` excluded from GZip (B-46), JSON at level 6; every catalog p95 within budget (stars 23.8 ms, dso 39.7 ms) |
| R41 | `tuple[...]` fields emit `prefixItems` | verified: openapi-typescript 7.13 emits TypeScript tuples |
| R42 | `/sky/frame` p95 >= 150 ms with 10 bodies x 32 samples | Retired 2026-09-06: p95 31.9 ms at concurrency 1 (105 ms at concurrency 4 on one worker) |
| R43 | 100 minor bodies: the per-body path measured about 780 ms warm | D69 batched propagation (100 bodies x 32 samples: 1130 ms -> 236 ms in-process); through the API p95 285 ms warm at concurrency 1, 1.8 s for the first request with a cold orbit cache (100 Parquet row reads); parity within 7e-15 |
| R44 | Rate-limit tests flaky on a slow CI; the shared client or the bench trips the limiter | rps 0.01 / burst 2 for the 429 test; the session app and the bench server run with rps 1e6 |
| R45 | A permanently failing bootstrap keeps the process alive with `503 starting` | intended: operators read `detail`; Docker's healthcheck (M7) restarts on its own policy (ADR-0008). M7 (2026-09-28, D200, ADR-0008 amendment): Docker never restarts an unhealthy container; `up --wait` exits 1 and the operator restarts |
| R46 | `SkyState`/`Bootstrap` shared across request threads | every published object is frozen; one immutable `Snapshot` swapped under a lock; `FrameCache` and the limiter hold their own locks |
| R47 | Multi-worker bootstrap races on downloads and cache builds | exclusive `flock` on `DATA_DIR/.bootstrap.lock` around the download and build stages; losers re-check before loading |
| R48 | Batched propagation diverges from the per-body path | parity test at 1e-9; `observe()` iterates light time per element, so the stacked call is the same computation |
| R49 | Headless WebGPU on the GitHub runner may expose no adapter with `--enable-unsafe-webgpu --use-webgpu-adapter=swiftshader` (unmeasured; measuring needs a push) | `chromium-webgpu` configured with `testMatch`, out of the CI list; maintainer question 16; the WebGPU rendering path is verified through Brave on the Windows host |
| R50 | Chromium deprecates the automatic SwiftShader WebGL fallback | Playwright still passes `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader`; tracked in `docs/testing.md` |
| R51 | WebKit throttles `history.replaceState` (100 calls per 10 s) | 2 Hz trailing throttle, identical strings skipped |
| R52 | CDS Hipparcos download slow or unavailable in the CI e2e job | registry fallback URLs, `actions/cache` of `data/`, `timeout-minutes: 30` (45 since 2026-09-30, R110) |
| R53 | Vitest 5 adopted later without re-checking jest-dom and the coverage include pattern | section 7 row and the ADR-0004 amendment carry the trigger and both required changes |
| R54 | Fresh selector objects loop under zustand 5 | `useShallow` in components, equality functions in engine subscriptions, memoised `urlStateOf` |
| R55 | StrictMode double effect creates two engines while the WebGPU init is pending | `AbortController` checked after every `await`, dispose on cleanup, unit test with a fake factory |
| R56 | `@types/node` in the app project types `setTimeout` as `NodeJS.Timeout` | `window.setTimeout`/`window.clearTimeout` in browser code; `tsc -b` verifies |
| R57 | 16 MB of star buffers and the geometry extent pass over 471,820 vertices on phones and within the 300 ms budget | parse and upload time measured; fallback: upload a magnitude prefix (the catalog is sorted) |
| R58 | The `codeSplitting` group regex misses Rolldown module ids on Windows paths or a future Babylon layout | verified on the built manifest; the `babylon` group catches everything else |
| R59 | The Playwright MCP browser (Brave on Windows) may be unreachable for the local verification | the steps are written in `docs/testing.md` for the human; the fps row states which path produced the number |
| R60 | A frame-buffer edge case (snapshot refetch loop, negative-speed seam) only shows at runtime | `decide` unit-tested with a fake clock for both signs, snapshot mode and the 1e-8 tolerance; the browser check plays at 3600x and -3600x |
| R61 | Two full-screen alpha passes (sky, ground) at DPR 2 on a 2022 phone | meshes hidden when `ground=off` or B = 0; measured on the host GPU; fallback `MAX_DEVICE_PIXEL_RATIO` 1.5 on coarse pointers (M6) |
| R62 | Label widths estimated vs real glyphs would make the non-overlap e2e assertion flaky | `measureText` cache per text; the spec asserts on the layout's own rects (`__sky.labels()`) |
| R63 | Picking over 117,955 stars at fov 1 deg per tap | ICRF cone pre-filter, < 5 ms; no GPU pick |
| R64 | Nominatim CORS on the public instance | permissive by default (`NOMINATIM_CORS_NOACCESSCONTROL`); one manual check in a browser, never from tests |
| R65 | Headless Chromium's answer to an un-granted geolocation prompt is undocumented | the denial path is tested through an `addInitScript` stub |
| R66 | e2e duration grows by 9-14 min for both CI projects | one boot per spec; measured figure recorded in `docs/testing.md`; `timeout-minutes: 30` kept (raised to 45 on 2026-09-30, R110) |
| R67 | CDP touch injection may not yield pointer events | `mouse.click` fallback in the mobile spec |
| R68 | The jsdom `<dialog>` polyfill gives no inert or top layer | the polyfill restores focus itself; focus return asserted in e2e |
| R69 | Clipboard under WebKit | Chromium-only clipboard assertions; WebKit falls back to the input path (manual project) |
| R70 | `frames.failing` appears only after the client's five retries (<= 7.5 s) | `degraded.spec` waits 30 s; an `onRetry` publication is an M6 option Closed at M6 (D161, R70 done): `RequestOptions.onRetry` publishes `frames.failing` from the first retry; the `degraded.spec.ts` alert stands within 10 s (measured 1.4-1.5 s after the first failed request, D-final report §3). |
| R71 | The Sun is up at `TT_FIXED` from Greenwich: the atmosphere would cull the probe stars | `appUrl` adds `atm=0`; the atmosphere probe sets `atm=1` explicitly |
| R72 | Skyfield's constellation map on apparent positions | `radec(epoch=B1875)` accepts any ICRF position; reference stars tested (Polaris UMi, Sirius CMa, M31 And) |
| R73 | Boundary chords sag up to ~1 deg on the longest edges | recorded (B-61); backend-side densification in B1875 is an M6 option Kept open at M6, merged with Q8 and B-61: interpolating great circles between the served J2000 corners would be as wrong as the chords (the IAU edges are constant RA/Dec in B1875); a correct densification needs a B1875 edge source (D161). |
| R74 | CoreLocation's heading axis when the phone is upright is undocumented | the compass axis is a parameter with hysteresis; checklist "turn on the spot upright, then flat"; the drag covers residuals |
| R75 | WebKit refuses `requestPermission()` after an `await` | synchronous helper inside the tap (D126); checklist "dialog on the first tap" |
| R76 | Android without a magnetometer or absolute events | relative fallback, manual-north hint, 3 s staleness |
| R77 | Sensor jitter and latency | `SMOOTHING_TAU_S` is one constant to tune on the device |
| R78 | Store writes at frame rate from the pose cost more than expected on a phone | `frameMs()` on the device; fallback = a preallocated pose buffer read by the engine (a recorded D80 exception) |
| R79 | An alpha canvas over the video at DPR 2 drops below 30 fps on a Pixel 6a class phone | `__sky.fps()` on the device; cap 1.5 on coarse pointers (R61, M6) |
| R80 | The coverage-alpha blend changes star crossovers on the black sky | before/after screenshots at integration; `uAr` AR-only switch as the fallback; maintainer question 27 |
| R81 | iOS swaps `videoWidth/videoHeight` on rotation | `resize` listener and the `loadedmetadata` wait |
| R82 | Rolldown `$initial`/priority semantics change on a bump | `check_chunks.mjs` fails the build; tooling rule text |
| R83 | A static import folds an AR module into the entry (`INEFFECTIVE_DYNAMIC_IMPORT`) | the gate requires every lazy source to be an `isDynamicEntry`; rule "types only" |
| R84 | The XR yaw sign or the alignment lands mirrored on a device | fixtures pin `basisFromBabylonQuaternion` against `RotationYawPitchRoll`; checklist "aim at the Moon"; the reference-space fallback is derived |
| R85 | `isSessionSupported` true without Google Play Services for AR (install prompt, ~10 s decline) | the copy names it as one possible cause; `entering` shown; the install flow foregrounds the Play Store, so the page goes hidden and the camera stream ends while `xr.phase === 'entering'`: the controller defers the camera restart until the entry settles (R94); checklist row 22 with a phone without ARCore |
| R86 | Transient activation expires while the XR chunk downloads | `preloadXr()` when `xr.support` becomes `supported` |
| R87 | Babylon restores the canvas at DPR 1 on session end | `resize()` in the session-end handler |
| R88 | A Babylon bump moves the WebXR ambient types | `tsc` fails loudly; then `@types/webxr` with an ADR |
| R89 | Off-axis XR projection on some device (symmetric assumed) | checklist |
| R90 | A top-layer `<dialog>` paints over the XR session | `requestAr` closes dialogs; checklist |
| R91 | A touch laptop passes the gate and starts the front webcam | `facingMode === 'user'` guard; `orientationUnavailable` after 3 s |
| R92 | The fake camera's resolution differs across Chromium binaries | the spec reads `videoWidth/videoHeight` from the hook, never hardcodes them |
| R93 | `ar.spec.ts` costs one extra worker and browser launch | measured at integration; `timeout-minutes: 30` kept (raised to 45 on 2026-09-30, R110) |
| R94 | Chrome ends (rather than mutes) the `getUserMedia` track when ARCore takes the camera for the `immersive-ar` session, or the page goes hidden while the entry is pending (R85): a plain `trackEnded` would leave AR a frame after the session opened | `arController.ts` treats an end while `ar.mode === 'xr'` or `ar.xr.phase === 'entering'` as a lost camera and calls `startCameraVideo` again on the first tick back in the idle sensor mode (a failed restart fails AR with the camera's code or `trackEnded`); four controller tests with the fake camera; device-only for the browser's actual behaviour: checklist rows 18 and 22 record which of mute or end happened |
| R95 | `selection.spec.ts` "follow mode keeps the selection at the centre" fails at the first attempt about one run in eight on SwiftShader (41-43 px instead of < 3, one to two frames of 3600x motion between the follow block's `setView` and the `screenOf` sample; measured identically on the M4 HEAD and on the M5 tree, so not an M5 regression; CI stays green on `retries: 1`) | M6: sample `screenOf`, the canvas box and `state().tt` inside one `page.evaluate` right after `waitForFrame`, or find and fix the one-tick lag in `SkyEngine.tick` (`sky/engine/SkyEngine.ts` follow block, `debug/skyDebug.ts::screenOf`); never widen the 3 px tolerance blindly Closed at M6 (D139, D162): follow writes `setView` every frame; `selection.spec.ts` samples after `afterFrames(1)`, two samples 500 ms apart; the follow step passed at the first attempt in every area run (A-final run 2: 46.4 / 41.5 s) and at Step F (57 passed / 7 skipped / 0 failed in 8.9 min (2026-09-24, two-block `a11y` file) and 58 passed / 8 skipped / 0 failed in 9.0 min on the final tree (2026-09-27, load 0.69); the one loaded run between them (load 18.7) had 3 starvation timeouts). |
| R96 | The `babylon-webgpu` chunk grew from 68.1 to 95.2 kB gzip because the XR graph makes Babylon's WGSL `default.*` material shaders reachable and the `ShadersWGSL` group regex captures them: WebGPU users download 27 kB of shaders nothing requests (WebGL2 users nothing) | M6 with the Node `import()` smoke green: narrow the regex to the engine modules or let the shaders fall out as their own lazy chunks (Q63); the 1.5 MB budget is far away Closed at M6 (D143, B-92): the regex without `ShadersWGSL` gives `babylon-webgpu` 66.2-66.3 kB gzip on Babylon 9.27.1 (96.2 kB before); check (g) keeps the material shader out. |

M7 (2026-09-28): the release checklist row exists as item 1 of `docs/architecture.md` "Deployment" (`docker compose exec web nginx -T \| grep connect-src` against `/api/v1/meta` `geocoder.url`); `check_images.mjs` (e) proves the rendering with `SKY_GEOCODER_ORIGIN=https://geocoder.example.org` and `check_nginx_headers.mjs` the Dockerfile default equal to `CSP_GEOCODER_ORIGIN_DEFAULT`; deriving one from the other in compose was declined (Q48 of the maintainer list, question 48); stays open as an operator check |

M7 (2026-09-30): the CI `timeout-minutes` raised from 30 to 45 after the CI reproduction of the milestone tree (2026-09-28, one worker, one retry) measured 18.6 min for the job with warm caches, the browser installed and no apt step (Playwright 16.3 min, Lighthouse 82 s, setup 46 s; 17.4 min at M6): a cold runner adds the installs from the action caches, the apt transaction of `playwright install --with-deps` and the artifact uploads, and a runner 1.3 times slower on the GPU-less rendering path would have reached 27-30 min; the first remote run's duration is re-read as `docs/testing.md` "End-to-end" says |

| R114 | At concurrency 4 with 100-body frames the per-worker anonymous RSS reaches 0.90-1.01 GB in flight (901.2 MB / `VmHWM` 973.1 MB on a fresh process, 989.6 / 1,077.2 and 1,010.3 MB in the two earlier records, 954.6 / 1,024.5 MB on a later concurrency-1 run of the same process) against the 1 GB of l.256, which passes at concurrency 1 (752.2 MB, 25 % headroom) | Recorded in the budgets table with both readings; the peak depends on how much of the orbit cache and the earlier transients the allocator still holds; M7: a container memory limit above 1 GB for a worker serving concurrent minor-body frames, or a bounded minor-body concurrency in the compute path; `bench_api.py --concurrency 4 --soak 20` reproduces it. M7 (2026-09-28, D187): the memory rule is 1 GB plus 1 GB per worker, `deploy.resources.limits.memory: ${SKY_API_MEMORY_LIMIT:-2G}` in `compose.yaml` (documented in `.env.example` and checklist item 6; maintainer question 46); no bounded concurrency in the compute path (no backend code in a build milestone); the first-start `memory.peak` of the acceptance run decides whether the 2G default moves before the second commit (R139): `memory.peak` 1,557,237,760 B about 30 s after the first `make up` with warm caches (page cache included; `anon` 771,280,896 B), 855,891,968-1,067,556,864 B on later warm starts, `memory.swap.current` 0; the cache-build peak is acceptance run 1's figure (the second commit); `memswap_limit` equals the limit since the rehearsal (Q97) |
| R115 | `catalogs/dso` p95 sits at 40-60 ms against the 50 ms budget on this CPU depending on the load (40.4 / 42.8 ms idle; 53.4-59.6 ms with a load of 2.5-9 and 1.4 GB of swap in use) because the 822,188 B JSON is gzipped on the fly to 133,162 B per request | Recorded with the load-average line the bench now prints under every table (D145); the idle pair is the passing record; a stored gzip in the API or `gzip_static` at M7 for the file-served catalogs would take the compression out of the p95 (backend owner, could). M7 (2026-09-28, D180, B-99): left to the API: nginx compresses nothing on the fly and `gzip_static` cannot apply to a proxied response, so the DSO JSON still travels as the API gzips it (probe 14) and the p95 is unchanged; the remedy stays a stored gzip in the API (could, post-M7) |
| R116 | Bind-mount ownership: a directory Docker creates for a missing bind source is root-owned and the uid-1000 api cannot write it | Mitigated (B-103, D194): `data/.gitkeep` tracked so a clone owns `data/`, `create_host_path: false` turns a missing directory into a daemon error, `SKY_UID`/`SKY_GID` for other hosts, the troubleshooting row for `PermissionError` |
| R117 | amd64 wheels add an estimated 15-30 MB to the api image, so the budget may pass here and fail in CI | Did not materialise (2026-09-28, area A): the amd64 image built under QEMU measures 379,547,713 B against 394,364,527 B on arm64, 14.8 MB smaller (the debian base layer is 87.6 MB on amd64 against 110 MB on arm64); the per-architecture figures stay in `docs/testing.md`, the runner's own figure follows the push |
| R118 | The site-packages trims edit uv-installed distributions (their `RECORD` files disagree) | The import smoke of `check_images.mjs` (i) (`skyapi.main`, `skyapi.cli.sky_data`, pandas, numpy, `pyarrow.parquet`, `pyarrow.dataset`, `pyarrow.fs`, `skyfield.api`) and `sky-data status` guard them on every `make images` and CI run; passed on arm64 on 2026-09-28 |
| R119 | `start_period 300s` masks a broken configuration for five minutes | `/health` `detail` and `docker compose logs api` answer at once; the healthcheck's verdict is proven under shortened flags in (g) |

| R121 | The slim nginx image has no curl | `wget -q --spider` (present in the slim busybox, Q93) against `/healthz`; every script probe goes through `node:http` |

| R123 | `useradd`/`groupadd` absent from a future slim variant | A loud build failure; `ARG PYTHON_IMAGE` allows a pinned fallback; verified present in trixie-slim |
| R124 | `node:24` tracks 24.x and could move past the lockfile's engine range | `engine-strict=true` in `.npmrc` fails `npm ci` loudly; the same floor as `.node-version` |
| R125 | The healthcheck probes appear in the API's access record every 30 s | Accepted (Q89): path only, no query string; the operator can filter `/api/v1/health` |
| R126 | Image-size semantics differ per store and the runner migrates to Ubuntu 26.04 (containerd store) between 2026-10-19 and 2026-11-19 | The `docker export` payload is the store-independent gate (D173); `.Size`, `Unpacked` and `Content` are printed, never gated |
| R127 | The stdlib ships no `.pyc` and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` on a read-only root recompiles it at every start | Measured (Q92): `import urllib.request` 0.190 s, `import skyapi.main` 1.116 s, ready 3.7-4.3 s in the container against 4.69 s on the host (de441) and the 60 s budget; accepted |
| R128 | An `HTTP(S)_PROXY` set for `sky-data fetch` would redirect the healthcheck probe | `urllib.request.build_opener(ProxyHandler({}))` ignores the environment proxies (D172) |
| R129 | nginx below 1.30.4 with a `map` regex and a non-cacheable variable (CVE-2026-42533) | The floor is noted in `frontend/nginx.conf`; `nginx:stable-alpine-slim` ships 1.30.5; `docker compose build --pull` before a release |
| R130 | http-level `error_log crit` hides upstream diagnostics | Accepted for privacy (D184); the main-context `error_log warn` keeps the start-up lines; maintainer question 43 may raise it to `error` for the first weeks |
| R131 | The SPA fallback answers 200 for any unknown path | By design (`try_files $uri /index.html`, probe 5); the dotfile regex and the docs pair answer 404 first |
| R132 | `return 301 https://$host$request_uri` drops a non-standard port | Documented in the runbook (TLS path A); production listens on 443 |
| R133 | `location ~ /\.` blocks HTTP-01 under `/.well-known/` | DNS-01 or an external ACME client assumed; an explicit `location ^~ /.well-known/acme-challenge/` before the dotfile rule is checklist item 3 (maintainer question 41) |
| R134 | Two Content-Security-Policy headers on `/api/*` (nginx's set plus the API's deny policy) | Browsers intersect the policies, nginx's wins for the page; +781 B per API response (D193); `url-state.spec.ts` unaffected (`transferSize` 300 on a 304) |
| R135 | A missing `.gz` sibling is silent (nginx serves the plain file) | Probe 2 and `check_images.mjs` (e) assert `content-encoding: gzip` on the HTML, the entry script, the stylesheet and the manifest; the image build writes 55 siblings |
| R136 | A future `mime.types` with `webmanifest` warns on the duplicate `types` entry | A warning, not a failure; the `types` block stays until the base's `mime.types` carries the entry |
| R137 | The 4 s / 5 s keep-alive race between nginx and uvicorn | GET-only requests, retried by the client (429/502/503/504); `keepalive_timeout 4s` under uvicorn's 5 s |
| R138 | IPv4-only `listen 80` (no `listen [::]:80`) | Recorded by probe 28 (`curl -6 http://[::1]/healthz`); default no IPv6 listener. Closed 2026-09-28: 204, Docker publishes `[::]:80->80/tcp` beside `0.0.0.0:80` and maps IPv6 host connections onto the IPv4-only listener |
| R139 | The first-start cache-build peak against the 2G default | Run 1 records `memory.peak`; the default moves before the second commit if the peak exceeds 1.8 GB (R114 row). Rehearsal 2026-09-28 (warm caches): 1,557,237,760 B right after the first `up` with the page cache of the mapped kernels included (`anon` 771,280,896 B), 855,891,968-1,067,556,864 B on later warm starts, `memory.swap.current` 0; `memswap_limit` now equals the limit (Q97). Acceptance 2026-09-30 (the clones, a first start that builds the caches on a fresh data directory): `memory.peak` 1,410,977,792 B and 1,388,142,592 B, 65.7 and 64.6 % of the limit (`max_rss_mb` 1305 and 1293 for the MPC build), 816,820,224 and 837,341,184 B after a warm restart and 1,409,384,448 / 1,082,970,112 B on de441: under 1.8 GB, the 2G default stays |
| R140 | `10.213.0.0/24` collides with a host network | `Pool overlaps` -> every use changed in `compose.override.yaml` (the subnet, `web`'s `ipv4_address` and `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS`, since 2026-09-28; the troubleshooting row); the anchors do not cross files |
| R141 | Playwright launches its own servers when 8000 or 4173 does not answer 2xx | The acceptance protocol curls both first (`compose.e2e.yaml` publishes them on loopback). Acceptance 2026-09-30: both answered 200 in each run and Playwright started no server |
| R142 | An unsized tmpfs counts toward the container's memory | Every tmpfs is sized (`/tmp` 64m; web 1m + 256m + 1m) |
| R143 | The web capability set is too narrow for the root master | Probe 27 (`stop` under 2 s, `nginx -s reload` without a `failed` line) proves `KILL`; the fallback is the default capability set. Note 2026-09-30 (measured; ADR-0025 amendment): the probe's two steps also pass on the web image started with the compose flags but without `KILL` (stop 0.475 s with exit code 0, a clean reload), because nginx commands its workers through their channels; the capability serves the master's `SIGKILL` of a worker that ignores a fast shutdown (`kill(29, 9) failed (1: Operation not permitted)` and a container still running after 12 s without it, a clean exit within 3.5 s with it), so probe 27 and `check_images.mjs` (e) prove the stop and the reload under the hardened set, and `KILL` stays for that escalation |
| R144 | Docker Hub anonymous pull limits on runner IPs | hadolint from ghcr; the four bases are pulled once per run; a login token is maintainer question 52 |
| R145 | gha cache eviction | Slower builds, never red (the cache is `cache-from`, the build does not need it) |
| R146 | A Windows-side listener on 80/443 is invisible to `ss` inside WSL | `netstat.exe -ano` in the troubleshooting row and the acceptance preconditions |
| R147 | The developer tree and the clone share `name: intersidera` | `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=intersidera-acc` for the acceptance run; `make down` in the developer tree first |
| R148 | Compose 2.38.2 / 5.1.3 (the runner today and after the migration) versus 5.3.1 here | Only keys verified on all three are used (D187); the CI job runs `config -q` on the base and both overlays without `.env` |
| R149 | One rate-limit bucket behind nginx for every Playwright worker (the host is one client, `10.213.0.1`) | A 429 in the e2e-through-nginx run is not a regression: the client retries; the count of record is the passed/skipped/failed triple. Rehearsal 2026-09-28: no 429 observed in either pass. Acceptance 2026-09-30: no 429 among the 1,109 and 1,176 requests nginx logged over the two-worker runs |
| R150 | Stack figures are Docker Desktop on WSL 2 figures (a VM, a bind mount through the Desktop file sharing) | Recorded as such in "Delivery (M7)"; the runner's and a Linux host's figures may differ |
| R151 | Node floats within 24.x (`.node-version` = `24`): a release can land between the milestone gates and a clone's gates | Seen on 2026-09-30: `make setup` in the acceptance clone installed v24.21.0 where the developer tree had run its gates on v24.20.0; by design (CI resolves the newest 24.x the same way), and every gate passed on both versions that day; pin an exact version only if a 24.x release breaks a gate |
| R152 | The native `make e2e` at its five default workers exhausts this 7.7 GB machine (the 2,048 MB swap full during the runs) | 2026-09-30: of the five-worker runs of the day two were green (10.0 min each) and three lost tests to starvation timeouts (one `expectRendered` predicate in the run-1 clone, two `centrePatch` polls on the fix tree, four in the run-2 clone: a `centrePatch` poll, two 5 s store polls and the 180 s axe scan; the R97 symptom), every one of them green alone or with one worker; the flake register's rules (the suite runs alone, never right after another suite; the CI settings, one worker, are the classification run: 60 / 8 / 0 in 19.7 min on the fix tree), one worker in CI, two workers for the compose path; no threshold or cap was changed |

## 9. Assumptions

- Outbound HTTPS stays available for PyPI, npm, GitHub, codeberg.org, NAIF, CDS/VizieR and `data.minorplanetcenter.net`; the `www.minorplanetcenter.net` timeout is specific to this network path and will be re-probed at M1.
- Development happens on aarch64 (WSL 2), CI and production images on amd64; every native dependency ships wheels or binaries for both, so no compiler is needed anywhere.
- `de440s.bsp` (1849-2150) is sufficient for every automated test; de441 is only ever fetched by a human or in production; `de440.bsp` is an optional middle tier for Moon-observer tests.
- No accounts, sessions, database or secrets exist; `.env` holds only non-secret `SKYAPI_*` values.
- The fnm default alias stays at v24.14.0; every Node invocation goes through `fnm exec --using=.node-version`, so the default is irrelevant to the build.
- Skyfield 1.55 runs on CPython 3.14.7 (upstream reports passing tests); verified as the first M1 task.
- The human performs the steps that need privileges or credentials: `sudo env "PATH=$PATH" fnm exec --using=24 npm --prefix frontend exec -- playwright install-deps`, `git push`, `sky-data fetch --full`, TLS certificates, the AR checklist on a real device. (Amended at M6, 2026-09-27: the agent ran `sky-data fetch --full` itself, a 3.3 GB resumed download that needs no privilege, and measured the de441 rows; the other items stand.) (Amended at M7, 2026-09-28: the M7 items are the TLS certificates and the deployment choice (nginx in tls mode with `SKY_TLS_DIR`, or an external proxy owning HSTS; maintainer questions 39-41), `git push` and the first remote CI run (the amd64 images and their sizes on the runner, `docker build --check` on Docker 28.0.4, Lighthouse, the weekly audit, the `webkit` and `chromium-webgpu` one-offs), and the production `.env` values (`SKYAPI_EPHEMERIS=de441.bsp`, `SKYAPI_WORKERS`, `SKY_API_MEMORY_LIMIT`, `SKYAPI_CORS_ORIGINS`, `SKYAPI_GEOCODER_EMAIL`, `SKY_GEOCODER_ORIGIN`; question 49); the agent registered no QEMU handler (Docker Desktop's own restart did, question 38).)
- Docker 29.6.2 and Compose v5.3.1 remain available locally for M7. (Confirmed at M7, 2026-09-28: present through Docker Desktop's WSL integration with the containerd image store and buildx 0.35.0-desktop.2; the amd64 images were built here under QEMU after Docker Desktop's restart registered the binfmt handlers, but their runtime proof is CI's: `import pyarrow` and `import pandas` segfault under `qemu-x86_64`, an emulator limitation, not an image defect; one Docker Desktop outage during the milestone (the daemon unreachable until the Desktop restart) cost the area A agent its session and its report was reconstructed from its logs.)
- The brief's line numbers cited throughout refer to `docs/brief.xml` v1.2 as committed at M0; the file is never edited.

Acceptance 2026-09-30, the compose stacks of the two clones: `bootstrap ready` 4.92 s and 4.79 s after `StartedAt` on de441 (within 1 s of the host figure) and 2.84-3.76 s on de440s: the decision stands. |

| Q98 | A prettier gate on YAML and Markdown at M7? | No; a post-M7 `docs(format)` pass. |
